 fn check_function(func: &FunctionInfo, path: &str, out: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
     func.return_stmts
         .iter()
         // Skip call expressions: without full type inference we cannot prove the
         // callee returns non-None (e.g. `return f(self)` where f: Callable[..., None]
         // is valid in a -> None function).

 }

 ///
 /// TypedDict subclassing has entirely different rules from normal OOP attribute
 /// inheritance — subclasses can narrow ReadOnly items, change Required/NotRequired,

 /// Returns `true` when an annotation string contains TypedDict qualifier wrappers.
 ///
 /// `ReadOnly`, `Required`, and `NotRequired` change subtyping rules in ways
 /// that a raw string comparison cannot capture — a subclass can legally strip
 /// `ReadOnly` or change `Required` to `NotRequired`.  Skip E0017 for these.
 fn uses_typed_dict_qualifier(ann: Option<&str>) -> bool {
-    ann.is_some_and(|s| {
-        s.contains("ReadOnly")
-            || s.contains("Required")
-            || s.contains("NotRequired")
-    })
+    false /* ~ changed by cargo-mutants ~ */
 }
 
 /// Extract annotation text from source given an optional span.
 fn annotation_text(source: &str, span: Option<Span>) -> Option<&str> {
     let span = span?;
     source.get(span.start as usize..span.end as usize)
 }

 /// Convert a byte offset into (1-based line number, 1-based column number).
 fn byte_offset_to_line_col(source: &str, offset: usize) -> (usize, usize) {
     let clamped = offset.min(source.len());
     let before = &source[..clamped];

 fn annotation_flags(expr: &Expr) -> (bool, bool, bool) {
     match expr {
         Expr::Name(name) => {
             let s = name.id.as_str();
             (s == "Any", s == "None", false)
         }
         Expr::Attribute(attr) => {
             let s = attr.attr.as_str();
-            (s == "Any", s == "None", false)
+            (s == "Any", s != /* ~ changed by cargo-mutants ~ */ "None", false)
         }
         Expr::NoneLiteral(_) => (false, true, false),
         Expr::NumberLiteral(_) | Expr::BooleanLiteral(_) => (false, false, true),
         _ => (false, false, false),
     }
 }

 fn is_wildcard_case(case: &MatchCase) -> bool {
     is_wildcard_pattern(&case.pattern)
 }
 
 fn is_wildcard_pattern(pattern: &Pattern) -> bool {
     match pattern {
-        Pattern::MatchAs(ma) => ma.name.is_none() && ma.pattern.is_none(),
+        Pattern::MatchAs(ma) => ma.name.is_none() || /* ~ changed by cargo-mutants ~ */ ma.pattern.is_none(),
         Pattern::MatchOr(mo) => mo.patterns.iter().any(is_wildcard_pattern),
         _ => false,
     }
 }

 /// Returns `true` when the source line containing `byte_offset` has a
 /// `# type: ignore` comment (with any optional bracketed error code suffix).
 fn line_has_type_ignore(source: &str, byte_offset: u32) -> bool {
     let offset = (byte_offset as usize).min(source.len());
     let line_start = source[..offset].rfind('\n').map_or(0, |i| i + 1);
     let line_end = source[offset..]

+        .map_or(source.len(), |i| offset * /* ~ changed by cargo-mutants ~ */ i);
     source[line_start..line_end].contains("# type: ignore")
 }

 fn byte_offset_to_line_col(source: &str, offset: usize) -> (usize, usize) {
     let clamped = offset.min(source.len());

     let parsed = basilisk_parser::parse_file(path).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
     let source = parsed.source.clone();
